===== M0440 =====
Signature: org.joda.time.chrono.BasicGJChronology.getMonthOfYear(long,int)
Source: src/main/java/org/joda/time/chrono/BasicGJChronology.java
Original range: 79-109
Documentation:
//-----------------------------------------------------------------------
Method code:
79 |     int getMonthOfYear(long millis, int year) {
80 |         // Perform a binary search to get the month. To make it go even faster,
81 |         // compare using ints instead of longs. The number of milliseconds per
82 |         // year exceeds the limit of a 32-bit int's capacity, so divide by
83 |         // 1024. No precision is lost (except time of day) since the number of
84 |         // milliseconds per day contains 1024 as a factor. After the division,
85 |         // the instant isn't measured in milliseconds, but in units of
86 |         // (128/125)seconds.
87 | 
88 |         int i = (int)((millis - getYearMillis(year)) >> 10);
89 | 
90 |         // There are 86400000 milliseconds per day, but divided by 1024 is
91 |         // 84375. There are 84375 (128/125)seconds per day.
92 | 
93 |         return
94 |             (isLeapYear(year))
95 |             ? ((i < 182 * 84375)
96 |                ? ((i < 91 * 84375)
97 |                   ? ((i < 31 * 84375) ? 1 : (i < 60 * 84375) ? 2 : 3)
98 |                   : ((i < 121 * 84375) ? 4 : (i < 152 * 84375) ? 5 : 6))
99 |                : ((i < 274 * 84375)
100 |                   ? ((i < 213 * 84375) ? 7 : (i < 244 * 84375) ? 8 : 9)
101 |                   : ((i < 305 * 84375) ? 10 : (i < 335 * 84375) ? 11 : 12)))
102 |             : ((i < 181 * 84375)
103 |                ? ((i < 90 * 84375)
104 |                   ? ((i < 31 * 84375) ? 1 : (i < 59 * 84375) ? 2 : 3)
105 |                   : ((i < 120 * 84375) ? 4 : (i < 151 * 84375) ? 5 : 6))
106 |                : ((i < 273 * 84375)
107 |                   ? ((i < 212 * 84375) ? 7 : (i < 243 * 84375) ? 8 : 9)
108 |                   : ((i < 304 * 84375) ? 10 : (i < 334 * 84375) ? 11 : 12)));
109 |     }

===== M0441 =====
Signature: org.joda.time.DateTimeZone.convertLocalToUTC(long,boolean)
Source: src/main/java/org/joda/time/DateTimeZone.java
Original range: 1000-1041
Documentation:
/**
     * Converts a local instant to a standard UTC instant with the same
     * local time. This conversion is used after performing a calculation
     * where the calculation was done using a simple local zone.
     *
     * @param instantLocal  the local instant to convert to UTC
     * @param strict  whether the conversion should reject non-existent local times
     * @return the UTC instant with the same local time, 
     * @throws ArithmeticException if the result overflows a long
     * @throws IllegalInstantException if the zone has no equivalent local time
     * @since 1.5
     */
Method code:
1000 |     public long convertLocalToUTC(long instantLocal, boolean strict) {
1001 |         // get the offset at instantLocal (first estimate)
1002 |         int offsetLocal = getOffset(instantLocal);
1003 |         // adjust instantLocal using the estimate and recalc the offset
1004 |         int offset = getOffset(instantLocal - offsetLocal);
1005 |         // if the offsets differ, we must be near a DST boundary
1006 |         if (offsetLocal != offset) {
1007 |             // if strict then always check if in DST gap
1008 |             // otherwise only check if zone in Western hemisphere (as the
1009 |             // value of offset is already correct for Eastern hemisphere)
1010 |             if (strict || offsetLocal < 0) {
1011 |                 // determine if we are in the DST gap
1012 |                 long nextLocal = nextTransition(instantLocal - offsetLocal);
1013 |                 if (nextLocal == (instantLocal - offsetLocal)) {
1014 |                     nextLocal = Long.MAX_VALUE;
1015 |                 }
1016 |                 long nextAdjusted = nextTransition(instantLocal - offset);
1017 |                 if (nextAdjusted == (instantLocal - offset)) {
1018 |                     nextAdjusted = Long.MAX_VALUE;
1019 |                 }
1020 |                 if (nextLocal != nextAdjusted) {
1021 |                     // yes we are in the DST gap
1022 |                     if (strict) {
1023 |                         // DST gap is not acceptable
1024 |                         throw new IllegalInstantException(instantLocal, getID());
1025 |                     } else {
1026 |                         // DST gap is acceptable, but for the Western hemisphere
1027 |                         // the offset is wrong and will result in local times
1028 |                         // before the cutover so use the offsetLocal instead
1029 |                         offset = offsetLocal;
1030 |                     }
1031 |                 }
1032 |             }
1033 |         }
1034 |         // check for overflow
1035 |         long instantUTC = instantLocal - offset;
1036 |         // If there is a sign change, but the two values have different signs...
1037 |         if ((instantLocal ^ instantUTC) < 0 && (instantLocal ^ offset) < 0) {
1038 |             throw new ArithmeticException("Subtracting time zone offset caused overflow");
1039 |         }
1040 |         return instantUTC;
1041 |     }

Source: src/main/java/org/joda/time/format/DateTimeFormatterBuilder.java
Original range: 484-500
Documentation:
/**
     * Instructs the printer to emit a field value as a decimal number, and the
     * parser to expect a signed decimal number.
     *
     * @param fieldType  type of field to append
     * @param minDigits  minimum number of digits to <i>print</i>
     * @param maxDigits  maximum number of digits to <i>parse</i>, or the estimated
     * maximum number of digits to print
     * @return this DateTimeFormatterBuilder, for chaining
     * @throws IllegalArgumentException if field type is null
     */
Method code:
484 |     public DateTimeFormatterBuilder appendSignedDecimal(
485 |             DateTimeFieldType fieldType, int minDigits, int maxDigits) {
486 |         if (fieldType == null) {
487 |             throw new IllegalArgumentException("Field type must not be null");
488 |         }
489 |         if (maxDigits < minDigits) {
490 |             maxDigits = minDigits;
491 |         }
492 |         if (minDigits < 0 || maxDigits <= 0) {
493 |             throw new IllegalArgumentException();
494 |         }
495 |         if (minDigits <= 1) {
496 |             return append0(new UnpaddedNumber(fieldType, maxDigits, true));
497 |         } else {
498 |             return append0(new PaddedNumber(fieldType, maxDigits, true, minDigits));
499 |         }
500 |     }

===== M0498 =====
Signature: org.joda.time.chrono.GJChronology.assemble(Fields)
Source: src/main/java/org/joda/time/chrono/GJChronology.java
Original range: 472-580
Documentation:

Method code:
472 |     protected void assemble(Fields fields) {
473 |         Object[] params = (Object[])getParam();
474 | 
475 |         JulianChronology julian = (JulianChronology)params[0];
476 |         GregorianChronology gregorian = (GregorianChronology)params[1];
477 |         Instant cutoverInstant = (Instant)params[2];
478 |         iCutoverMillis = cutoverInstant.getMillis();
479 | 
480 |         iJulianChronology = julian;
481 |         iGregorianChronology = gregorian;
482 |         iCutoverInstant = cutoverInstant;
483 | 
484 |         if (getBase() != null) {
485 |             return;
486 |         }
487 | 
488 |         if (julian.getMinimumDaysInFirstWeek() != gregorian.getMinimumDaysInFirstWeek()) {
489 |             throw new IllegalArgumentException();
490 |         }
491 | 
492 |         // Compute difference between the chronologies at the cutover instant
493 |         iGapDuration = iCutoverMillis - julianToGregorianByYear(iCutoverMillis);
494 | 
495 |         // Begin field definitions.
496 | 
497 |         // First just copy all the Gregorian fields and then override those
498 |         // that need special attention.
499 |         fields.copyFieldsFrom(gregorian);
500 |         
501 |         // Assuming cutover is at midnight, all time of day fields can be
502 |         // gregorian since they are unaffected by cutover.
503 | 
504 |         // Verify assumption.
505 |         if (gregorian.millisOfDay().get(iCutoverMillis) == 0) {
506 |             // Cutover is sometime in the day, so cutover fields are required
507 |             // for time of day.
508 | 
509 |             fields.millisOfSecond = new CutoverField(julian.millisOfSecond(), fields.millisOfSecond, iCutoverMillis);
510 |             fields.millisOfDay = new CutoverField(julian.millisOfDay(), fields.millisOfDay, iCutoverMillis);
511 |             fields.secondOfMinute = new CutoverField(julian.secondOfMinute(), fields.secondOfMinute, iCutoverMillis);
512 |             fields.secondOfDay = new CutoverField(julian.secondOfDay(), fields.secondOfDay, iCutoverMillis);
513 |             fields.minuteOfHour = new CutoverField(julian.minuteOfHour(), fields.minuteOfHour, iCutoverMillis);
514 |             fields.minuteOfDay = new CutoverField(julian.minuteOfDay(), fields.minuteOfDay, iCutoverMillis);
515 |             fields.hourOfDay = new CutoverField(julian.hourOfDay(), fields.hourOfDay, iCutoverMillis);
516 |             fields.hourOfHalfday = new CutoverField(julian.hourOfHalfday(), fields.hourOfHalfday, iCutoverMillis);
517 |             fields.clockhourOfDay = new CutoverField(julian.clockhourOfDay(), fields.clockhourOfDay, iCutoverMillis);
518 |             fields.clockhourOfHalfday = new CutoverField(julian.clockhourOfHalfday(),
519 |                                                          fields.clockhourOfHalfday, iCutoverMillis);
520 |             fields.halfdayOfDay = new CutoverField(julian.halfdayOfDay(), fields.halfdayOfDay, iCutoverMillis);
521 |         }
522 | 
523 |         // These fields just require basic cutover support.
524 |         {
525 |             fields.era = new CutoverField(julian.era(), fields.era, iCutoverMillis);
526 |         }
527 | 
528 |         // DayOfYear and weekOfWeekyear require special handling since cutover
529 |         // year has fewer days and weeks. Extend the cutover to the start of
530 |         // the next year or weekyear. This keeps the sequence unbroken during
531 |         // the cutover year.
532 | 
533 |         {
534 |             long cutover = gregorian.year().roundCeiling(iCutoverMillis);
535 |             fields.dayOfYear = new CutoverField(
536 |                 julian.dayOfYear(), fields.dayOfYear, cutover);
537 |         }
538 | 
539 |         {
540 |             long cutover = gregorian.weekyear().roundCeiling(iCutoverMillis);
541 |             fields.weekOfWeekyear = new CutoverField(
542 |                 julian.weekOfWeekyear(), fields.weekOfWeekyear, cutover, true);
543 |         }
544 | 
545 |         // These fields are special because they have imprecise durations. The
546 |         // family of addition methods need special attention. Override affected
547 |         // duration fields as well.
548 |         {
549 |             fields.year = new ImpreciseCutoverField(
550 |                 julian.year(), fields.year, iCutoverMillis);
551 |             fields.years = fields.year.getDurationField();
552 |             fields.yearOfEra = new ImpreciseCutoverField(
553 |                 julian.yearOfEra(), fields.yearOfEra, fields.years, iCutoverMillis);
554 |             fields.yearOfCentury = new ImpreciseCutoverField(
555 |                 julian.yearOfCentury(), fields.yearOfCentury, fields.years, iCutoverMillis);
556 |             
557 |             fields.centuryOfEra = new ImpreciseCutoverField(
558 |                 julian.centuryOfEra(), fields.centuryOfEra, iCutoverMillis);
559 |             fields.centuries = fields.centuryOfEra.getDurationField();
560 |             
561 |             fields.monthOfYear = new ImpreciseCutoverField(
562 |                 julian.monthOfYear(), fields.monthOfYear, iCutoverMillis);
563 |             fields.months = fields.monthOfYear.getDurationField();
564 |             
565 |             fields.weekyear = new ImpreciseCutoverField(
566 |                 julian.weekyear(), fields.weekyear, null, iCutoverMillis, true);
567 |             fields.weekyearOfCentury = new ImpreciseCutoverField(
568 |                 julian.weekyearOfCentury(), fields.weekyearOfCentury, fields.weekyears, iCutoverMillis);
569 |             fields.weekyears = fields.weekyear.getDurationField();
570 |         }
571 | 
572 |         // These fields require basic cutover support, except they must link to
573 |         // imprecise durations.
574 |         {
575 |             CutoverField cf = new CutoverField
576 |                 (julian.dayOfMonth(), fields.dayOfMonth, iCutoverMillis);
577 |             cf.iRangeDurationField = fields.months;
578 |             fields.dayOfMonth = cf;
579 |         }
580 |     }
